People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Yeah… I think so, at least on those nights. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but when it’s after midnight and I get takeout or start on snacks, I kind of tear through it pretty fast before I’m really paying attention.

I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it, but that’s the part that kind of scared me into coming in.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after eating, especially after those late-night snacks?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I mean, not so much guilty about the food itself, I guess. It’s more like after I eat late, I start thinking about everything I’ve messed up.\n\nLike I keep replaying mistakes I made with those Providence accounts at work, and then it turns into this whole thing where I’m thinking I blew my future and I’m useless, and probably everybody at work can see it. I know that might sound dramatic… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But yeah, that guilt kind of hits me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're eating an amount of food that's definitely more than most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe? I don’t want to make it sound like it’s some huge thing, but there are nights where it feels like more than a normal snack, yeah. Like after midnight I’ll end up going through takeout and snacks, and afterward I’m kind of like, “Okay, that was probably a lot.”  \n\nBut it’s not every day. Other days I honestly forget meals completely, so it’s just been really all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have control over what or how much you're eating during those late-night snacking episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… not as much as I’d like to say. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m totally out of control, because it’s not like that every night, but once I start after midnight, with takeout or snacks, I kind of just keep going and don’t really think about it until after.\n\nThen other days it’s the opposite, like I’ll forget meals completely. So it’s just been really all over the place, honestly. That’s part of why it worried me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during those late-night snacking episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but on those nights after midnight, once I start with takeout or snacks, I can kind of tear through it before I really stop and think.\n\nIt’s weird because some days I forget meals completely, so it’s not like I’m eating like that all the time. It’s just been really all over the place, and honestly that’s part of what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for what you've eaten by doing something to counteract it, like exercising or vomiting?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean… I might tell myself I’ll eat lighter the next day or something, but I don’t make myself throw up or go work out to “undo” it. Nothing like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating very quickly during those late-night snacking episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, at least on those nights. Some days I honestly forget meals completely, so the whole eating thing has just been really all over the place. That’s what scared me enough to come in, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat late at night when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure it’s always because I’m stressed or anxious, honestly. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nIt’s more like… my eating has just been really inconsistent. Some days I’ll forget meals almost completely, and then other nights, after midnight, I’m tearing through takeout or snacks. I don’t want to make it sound like it’s some huge thing, but there are nights where it feels like more than a normal snack, yeah. Other days I honestly forget meals completely, so it’s just been really all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have control over what or how much you're eating during those late-night snacking episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… not as much as I’d like to say. It’s just been really all over the place, and honestly that’s part of what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for what you've eaten by doing something to counteract it, like exercising or vomiting?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it, but that’s the part that kind of scared me into coming in.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after eating, especially after those late-night snacks?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I mean, not so much guilty about the food itself, I guess. It’s more like after I eat late, I start thinking about everything I’ve messed up.\n\nLike I keep replaying mistakes I made with those Providence accounts at work, and then it turns into this whole thing where I’m thinking I blew my future and I’m useless, and probably everybody at work can see it. I know that might sound dramatic… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But yeah, that guilt kind of hits me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're eating an amount of food that's definitely more than most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe? I don’t want to make it sound like it’s some huge thing, but there are nights where it feels like more than a normal snack, yeah. Like after midnight I’ll end up going through takeout and snacks, and afterward I’m kind of like, “Okay, that was probably a lot.”  \n\nBut it’s not every day. Other days I honestly forget meals completely, so it’s just been really all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have control over what or how much you're eating during those late-night snacking episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… not as much as I’d like to say. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m totally out of control, because it’s not like that every night, but once I start after midnight, with takeout or snacks, I kind of just keep going and don’t really think about it until after.\n\nThen other days it’s the opposite, like I’ll forget meals completely. It’s just been really all over the place, and honestly that’s part of what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for what you've eaten by doing something to counteract it, like exercising or vomiting?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m totally out of control, because it’s not like that every night, but once I start after midnight, with takeout or snacks, I kind of just keep going and don’t really think about it until after.\n\nThen other days it’s the opposite, like I’ll forget meals completely. So it’s just been really all over the place, honestly. That’s part of why it worried me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during those late-night snacking episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but on those nights after midnight, once I start with takeout or snacks, I can kind of tear through it before I really stop and think.\n\nIt’s weird because some days I forget meals completely, so it’s not like I’m eating like that all the time. It’s just been really all over the place, and honestly that’s part of what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for what you've eaten by doing something to counteract it, like exercising or vomiting?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean… I might tell myself I’ll eat lighter the next day or something, but I don’t make myself throw up or go work out to “undo” it. Nothing like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating very quickly during those late-night snacking episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, at least on those nights. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but when it’s after midnight and I get takeout or start on snacks, I kind of tear through it pretty fast before I’m really paying attention.\n\nIt’s not every day, though. Some days I honestly forget meals completely, so the whole eating thing has just been really all over the place. That’s what scared me enough to come in, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat late at night when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure it’s always because I’m stressed or anxious, honestly. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nIt’s more like… my eating has just been really inconsistent. Some days I’ll forget meals almost completely, and then other nights, after midnight, I’m tearing through takeout or snacks. That up-and-down part is what kind of scared me into coming in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty specifically about eating too much, rather than about other things in your life?\n\nPatient: Not really about eating too much, no. I mean, I don’t feel great after those nights, but the guilt is more… other stuff.\n\nMostly work. I keep replaying mistakes I made with those Providence accounts, and I start thinking I blew my whole future over it. Like I’m just useless and everybody at work can see it. I know that probably sounds kind of dramatic, but that’s more what the guilt is about, if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat more or less when you're feeling upset or emotional?\n\nPatient: I guess it can go either way, if that makes sense. When I’m upset or just emotional, sometimes I don’t eat much at all and kind of forget meals. That swing is what scared me, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating without being able to control how much you're eating during those late-night snacking episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely out of control or anything, but on those nights it kind of feels that way.\n\nHonestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is just that my eating has been all over the place. Some days I forget meals completely, and then other nights after midnight I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I’m not really keeping track. It’s not, like, every single night, but it’s happened enough that it started to scare me.\n\nSome days I’ll barely eat or forget meals, and then other nights after midnight I’m suddenly going through takeout or snacks way more than I meant to. So I don’t know the exact number, but it feels like a real pattern, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week would you say you have one of these late-night eating episodes where you feel like you're eating a lot without being able to control it?\n\nPatient: Maybe… a couple times a week? You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I don’t love admitting that, but sometimes I do feel pretty bad about it afterward. Like I should’ve had it more together.

Honestly, the eating being so all over the place is what scared me into coming in — some days I forget meals completely, and then other nights I’m tearing through takeout or snacks after midnight. Then afterward I’m kind of sitting there like, “Why did I do that?” If that makes sense.
